00:07:58 --> 00:08:04
France is Paris the Decay happens less

00:08:01 --> 00:08:07
teeply so for example if we move this

00:08:03 --> 00:08:09
interactive

00:08:06 --> 00:08:12
[Music]

00:08:08 --> 00:08:14
graph you'll notice that the lines are

00:08:11 --> 00:08:17
getting less and less steep right at the

00:08:13 --> 00:08:20
start if we look at the very first

00:08:16 --> 00:08:22
one that is a very kind of steep drop

00:08:19 --> 00:08:25
off this is exactly how memory works

00:08:21 --> 00:08:27
it's a steep exponential curve but if we

00:08:24 --> 00:08:30
recall it

00:08:26 --> 00:08:33
here and over there

00:08:29 --> 00:08:35
and over there we see

00:08:32 --> 00:08:37
that actually it takes us longer to

00:08:34 --> 00:08:38
forget the same amount of information

00:08:36 --> 00:08:40
which is why you never forget that the

00:08:37 --> 00:08:42
capital of France is Paris because you

00:08:39 --> 00:08:44
just come across that fact so often that

00:08:41 --> 00:08:46
it's now memorized forever and that's

00:08:43 --> 00:08:48
the idea behind space repetition um

00:08:45 --> 00:08:51
here's a nice little graph and if we hit

00:08:47 --> 00:08:53
he auto optimize button if we want to

00:08:50 --> 00:08:54
kind of optimize our memory to stay in

00:08:52 --> 00:08:56
this kind of yellow Sweet Spot you'll

00:08:53 --> 00:08:59
see that the the intervals that we are

00:08:55 --> 00:09:00
peating it at gradually lengthen and

00:08:58 --> 00:09:03
the idea is that because as we we repeat

00:09:00 --> 00:09:04
he topics and as we repeat that factual

00:09:02 --> 00:09:06
recall we strengthen the connections and

00:09:03 --> 00:09:08
then it takes us longer to forget it but

00:09:05 --> 00:09:10
it's the fact that we've forgotten it

00:09:07 --> 00:09:12
and then we're recalling it that is what

00:09:09 --> 00:09:15
allows the memory to take hold because

00:09:11 --> 00:09:17
the brain as I say in my previous class

00:09:14 --> 00:09:19
is sort of like a muscle in that when

00:09:16 --> 00:09:21
it's working hard it's growing but if

00:09:18 --> 00:09:23
it's working too easily it's not growing

00:09:20 --> 00:09:25
so I could read Capital Francis Paris

00:09:22 --> 00:09:26
over and over again in a single study

00:09:24 --> 00:09:28
session but my brain wouldn't be working

00:09:25 --> 00:09:31
because I haven't forgotten that fact

00:09:27 --> 00:09:32
whereas over time as I allow my bra to

00:09:30 --> 00:09:34
forget some of the stuff and then I

00:09:31 --> 00:09:36
recall it that's like the magical way to

00:09:33 --> 00:09:38
remember every anything forever so this

easy medium or or hard Anki has this

pecial spaced repetition algorithm uh

that's modeled after a service called

basically it automatically figures out

00:13:28 --> 00:13:33

days time if I found it hard it would

appropriately based on when I'm most

information in once a reversed card

00:39:34 --> 00:39:38
allows us to create double the number of

00:39:36 --> 00:39:39
lash cards so that instead of just

00:39:37 --> 00:39:41
esting a one-way Association we're

00:39:38 --> 00:39:42

be important for you depending on what

in medicine in some contexts not in

other context there are some areas in

that in a question I'd want to

00:40:01 --> 00:40:05
immediately be able to associate that

00:40:03 --> 00:40:07
with Cola so it's helpful to have the

00:40:04 --> 00:40:08
Reversed card in that sense so that's

what they're for sometimes you might

00:40:07 --> 00:40:11

single most powerful feature of an so

01:47:19 --> 01:47:23
you don't really need to know the

details basically the new card def def

01:47:22 --> 01:47:29
ault steps I think are 3 and 15 which

means that when a card is new you'll

01:47:28 --> 01:47:33
have noticed in the previous examples if

you click again it comes up in 3 minutes

01:47:32 --> 01:47:36
and if you click good it comes up in 15

minutes and then after that time if you

01:47:35 --> 01:47:40
click good it graduates because the

01:47:37 --> 01:47:43
gradu graduating interval is set to one

01:47:39 --> 01:47:45
day so after just getting it right twice

01:47:42 --> 01:47:47
in a span of 15 minutes the card counts

01:47:44 --> 01:47:50
as having graduated and then if you ever

01:47:46 --> 01:47:52
get it wrong again at that point the

01:47:49 --> 01:47:53
card difficulty becomes penalized which

01:47:51 --> 01:47:55
means that you start seeing that card

01:47:52 --> 01:47:57
over and over again and in this video

01:47:54 --> 01:47:58
konana calls it ease hell and I think

01:47:56 --> 01:48:00
that's a great a great description of it

01:47:57 --> 01:48:02
because everyone who doesn't change the

default anky settings gets into this

problem where they're like I I swear

I've been seeing this card so often why

does it keep on coming up it's cuz you

01:48:05 --> 01:48:08
haven't changed the settings these are

01:48:06 --> 01:48:10
the settings you want to change it to so

01:48:07 --> 01:48:12
for new cards you want to write 15 so

01:48:09 --> 01:48:13
the first interval is 15 minutes 3

01:48:11 --> 01:48:14
minutes is a bit pointless because 3

01:48:12 --> 01:48:15
minutes you're not really learning

01:48:13 --> 01:48:17
anything you're just testing your

01:48:14 --> 01:48:21
short-term retention of it the next so

01:48:16 --> 01:48:22
15 space 1440 1440 is 1440 minutes to

01:48:20 --> 01:48:24
ne day that means the first interval is

01:48:21 --> 01:48:27
15 minutes the next interval is 1 day

01:48:23 --> 01:48:29
and the third interval 8640 which I

01:48:26 --> 01:48:32
think is six 6 days I think that's 6

01:48:28 --> 01:48:33
days so that's 15 minutes 1 day 6 days

01:48:31 --> 01:48:35
and the graduating interval is also

01:48:32 --> 01:48:36
really important to change we want to

01:48:34 --> 01:48:38
change that to 15 days now what this

01:48:35 --> 01:48:40
means is that the first time we see a

01:48:37 --> 01:48:42
card if it's good it comes up in 15

01:48:39 --> 01:48:44
minutes if it's good then it comes up in

01:48:41 --> 01:48:46
a day then it comes up in 6 days then it

01:48:43 --> 01:48:50
comes up in 15 days and after we've got

01:48:46 --> 01:48:52
it right after 15 days of it being a new

01:48:49 --> 01:48:53
card then that card graduates and at

01:48:51 --> 01:48:55
hat point we're pretty confident we

01:48:52 --> 01:48:56
kind of know what's on the card and at

01:48:54 --> 01:48:58
hat point if we get it wrong then we

01:48:55 --> 01:48:59
start penalizing ourselves which we

01:48:57 --> 01:49:01
won't go into it's all explained in the

01:48:58 --> 01:49:03
video but with the default settings you

01:49:00 --> 01:49:05
don't really get to know a card very

01:49:02 --> 01:49:06
well you just have it in your short-term

01:49:04 --> 01:49:07
emory and for a lot of us we have

01:49:05 --> 01:49:09
pretty good short-term memories I've got

01:49:06 --> 01:49:10
pretty good short-term memories it

01:49:08 --> 01:49:12
becomes very easy to just graduate a

01:49:09 --> 01:49:14
card whereas you want to actually get

01:49:11 --> 01:49:15
more and more steps until a card becomes

01:49:13 --> 01:49:18
graduated because when anky marks a card

01:49:14 --> 01:49:19
is graduated Anki is saying that we

01:49:17 --> 01:49:21
xpect you to have memorized this card

01:49:18 --> 01:49:22
by this point whereas like you know

01:49:20 --> 01:49:24
there's cards that I've got tomorrow

01:49:21 --> 01:49:25
that are capital of Andor that I really

01:49:23 --> 01:49:26
can't remember or capital of Armenia

01:49:24 --> 01:49:27
that I can't remember I wouldn't want

01:49:25 --> 01:49:29
hat to count as graduated cuz I know I

01:49:26 --> 01:49:31
can't remember it like I I want to have

01:49:28 --> 01:49:33
recalled it four times with at least 15

01:49:30 --> 01:49:34
days of a gap between them to know that

01:49:32 --> 01:49:36
I fully know the card before it becomes

01:49:33 --> 01:49:38
a graduated card so these are one of the

01:49:35 --> 01:49:39
most important settings to do show new

01:49:37 --> 01:49:41
cards in the order added I actually

01:49:38 --> 01:49:43
prefer show new cards in a random order

01:49:40 --> 01:49:45
because as you saw in the previous

01:49:42 --> 01:49:46
examples as we were creating capitals it

01:49:44 --> 01:49:48
becomes easy to actually memorize the

01:49:45 --> 01:49:50
order of the cards just subconsciously

01:49:47 --> 01:49:51
so adding new cards in a random order

01:49:49 --> 01:49:53
means that you don't see one after the

01:49:51 --> 01:49:54
other so you can figure out what the

01:49:52 --> 01:49:57
answer is based on the order that the

01:49:54 --> 01:49:59
answer came in so add new cards in a

01:49:56 --> 01:50:01
random order and finally for the easy

01:49:58 --> 01:50:04
interval I like to have 60 days as con

01:50:00 --> 01:50:05
recommends basically I tend not to hit

01:50:03 --> 01:50:07
easy I was hitting it a few times in the

01:50:04 --> 01:50:08
xample just just to kind of get us

01:50:06 --> 01:50:10
through the cards but I usually don't

arking it as easy then I'm really

questioning why do I actually have this

01:50:12 --> 01:50:16

like the capital Francis Paris would be

01:50:15 --> 01:50:19
an easy card it would be the sort of car

01:50:16 --> 01:50:20
that I actually don't need to make so I

01:50:18 --> 01:50:22
tend not to Mark easy but easy interval

01:50:19 --> 01:50:24
I would just put a 60 days so these are

01:50:21 --> 01:50:25
the first settings to change next when

01:50:23 --> 01:50:27
it comes to the reviews these are the

01:50:24 --> 01:50:29
settings I would recommend so maximum

01:50:26 --> 01:50:31
reviews per day I would set to 9999

01:50:28 --> 01:50:33
because basically you always want to do

01:50:30 --> 01:50:34
the highest number of reviews that Anki

01:50:32 --> 01:50:36
says you should because if I said

01:50:33 --> 01:50:38
maximum reviews 20 a day like I only

01:50:35 --> 01:50:40
want to revise 20 cards a day and I've

01:50:37 --> 01:50:41
got 500 cards then I'm I'm never going

01:50:39 --> 01:50:43
to get through them all because I'm only

01:50:40 --> 01:50:45
doing a maximum of 20 each day whereas I

01:50:42 --> 01:50:46
set it to 9 and99 because the Anki

01:50:44 --> 01:50:48
algorithm is very good it's it's very

01:50:45 --> 01:50:49
good at predicting when I'm going to

01:50:47 --> 01:50:51
forget stuff basically if you set your

01:50:48 --> 01:50:52
maximum reviews per day to too low a

01:50:50 --> 01:50:54
number you are hamstringing the

01:50:52 --> 01:50:55
algorithm you're not letting the

01:50:53 --> 01:50:57
algorithm work because you are not up

01:50:54 --> 01:50:58
for doing enough questions and sometimes

01:50:56 --> 01:50:59
when you're getting into the swing of

01:50:57 --> 01:51:01
things you can just Blitz through

01:50:58 --> 01:51:02
undreds of flash cards in a in a given

01:51:00 --> 01:51:04
sitting so I like to have reviews at

01:51:01 --> 01:51:07
9999 easy bonus is fine interval

01:51:03 --> 01:51:09
modifier is fine and maximum interval 90

01:51:06 --> 01:51:11
days is fine that's sort of the maximum

01:51:08 --> 01:51:13
amount of time that you'll see a card

01:51:10 --> 01:51:15
coming back in basically and then

01:51:12 --> 01:51:17
importantly for lapses this is important

01:51:14 --> 01:51:19
so essentially a lapse is what happens

01:51:16 --> 01:51:20
when you've got a graduated card and you

01:51:18 --> 01:51:22
get it wrong right so a card that has

01:51:19 --> 01:51:24
graduated in in Anki default settings a

01:51:21 --> 01:51:25
card that's graduated is a card that

01:51:23 --> 01:51:26
you've known for one day in my book that

01:51:24 --> 01:51:27
doesn't count as knowing something that

01:51:25 --> 01:51:29
just means you remember it from

01:51:26 --> 01:51:31
yesterday it doesn't count whereas in

01:51:28 --> 01:51:33
our new settings as we see over here a

01:51:30 --> 01:51:34
card that's graduated is a card that

01:51:32 --> 01:51:37
we've been able to recall four times

01:51:34 --> 01:51:39
with a 15-day gap between those recalls

01:51:36 --> 01:51:41
o if at that point we get it wrong then

01:51:38 --> 01:51:43
okay fair play we should start seeing

01:51:40 --> 01:51:44
that card more often the default step is

01:51:42 --> 01:51:46
15 minutes I think 20 minutes is better

01:51:43 --> 01:51:48
because really 15 minutes is not too too

01:51:45 --> 01:51:50
great new interval now I think that

01:51:47 --> 01:51:52
should be changed so what that means is

that basically if you get a card wrong

01:51:51 --> 01:51:55
once it's graduated it will completely

01:51:53 --> 01:51:57
reset back to its default and you're

going through your 15 minutes 1 day 6

days 15 days all over again which

becomes a real annoyance especially if

you made a typo or something or you

actually knew it but if but you just

sort of forgot it for a moment it's

unfair on on ourselves to make us then

redo that card in circulation again

because a it's it's not it's not a very

nice feeling and B it also comes at the

xpense of the other cards so I think

01:52:13 --> 01:52:18
it's useful to have that at 70% as

konana recommends so 70% minimum

01:52:17 --> 01:52:23
interval 2 days and Lee threshold eight

01:52:20 --> 01:52:25
lapses Lee action tag only it's really

01:52:22 --> 01:52:27
important that this should be tag only

01:52:24 --> 01:52:29
essentially what that means is that if

01:52:26 --> 01:52:31
we get a card wrong eight times in a row

01:52:28 --> 01:52:32
then anky will tag it and tell us that

01:52:30 --> 01:52:34
guy mate you've been getting this card

01:52:31 --> 01:52:36
wrong eight times in a row suspend is

01:52:33 --> 01:52:37
bad because suspend means Anki will

01:52:35 --> 01:52:39
start hiding that card and never show it

01:52:36 --> 01:52:40
again but if you've got something wrong

01:52:38 --> 01:52:42
eight times in a row you probably want

01:52:39 --> 01:52:45
o show it rather than hide it so tag

01:52:41 --> 01:52:46
only is the way for that and now I've

when I was in my second year we were

stuff using anky and me and my friends

01:54:50 --> 01:54:54
basically when it comes to reviewing a

d so my default thing is I scroll

review your flash cards what about when

you should create them now A friend of

he would take notes in the lecture and

from his like flash card generation and

so when you start getting good at using

he things you have to memorize the

a I'm kind of doing a bit of revision

tuff the better because then we're

fully taking advantage of active recall

and space repetition we're fully taking

advantage of the fact that you know our

brain is wired to remember things over a

long period of time provided we give it

on and I say this in the nicest possible

another question that we've had quite a

02:01:43 --> 02:01:48
lot of on on Instagram is what should I

02:01:52 --> 02:01:55
you're at what year you and how old you

02:01:53 --> 02:01:57
are what your exam is doing and what

02:01:55 --> 02:01:59
specific things you want to be trying to

02:01:56 --> 02:02:01
memorize basically I think the way to

02:01:58 --> 02:02:02

second brain it's like an extension of

your brain and anything that goes into

Ani will become uploaded to your brain

provided you kind of do it effectively

02:02:07 --> 02:02:12
and consistently and all that stuff and

02:02:09 --> 02:02:14
provided you keep you stick to the

02:02:11 --> 02:02:16
minimum information principle in general

02:02:13 --> 02:02:18
and have not too much information on

02:02:15 --> 02:02:20
each flash card preferably just one

02:02:17 --> 02:02:23
factoid one examinable fact so kind of

02:02:19 --> 02:02:24
with that mindset in mind that Ates what

you should put on a flash card as I said

in the previous video I wouldn't make a

know that it's an irrelevant fact maybe

it's kind of useful to appreciate how

of thing I want to memorize as a piece

of information and if I had too many of

02:02:37 --> 02:02:41
those going through my flash cards would

get really really dull and pointless

02:02:40 --> 02:02:43
because I'd be like oh this is a

02:02:41 --> 02:02:45
pointless question this is never going

02:02:42 --> 02:02:47
to come up in an exam and partly it also

02:02:44 --> 02:02:49
depends on what's our aim of using flash

02:02:46 --> 02:02:51
cards are we using flash cards purely to

02:02:48 --> 02:02:53
learn or are we using flash cards to

02:02:50 --> 02:02:54
prepare for an exam because everyone

02:02:52 --> 02:02:56
realizes this at some point in their

02:02:53 --> 02:02:59
lives and the sooner we can realize this

02:02:55 --> 02:03:00
the better our exams are not the arena

02:02:58 --> 02:03:02
where we test how much we've learned our

02:02:59 --> 02:03:04
exams are the arena where we test how

02:03:01 --> 02:03:06
ell we've prepared for the exam in an

02:03:03 --> 02:03:08
Ideal World in an absolutely perfect

02:03:05 --> 02:03:10
education system we would learn stuff

02:03:08 --> 02:03:11
and the exams would test the learning

02:03:09 --> 02:03:12
but and the exams would be relevant to

02:03:10 --> 02:03:14
the learning and maybe wouldn't even

02:03:11 --> 02:03:16
have exams at all but in the real world

02:03:13 --> 02:03:18
we have to play the game of exams so it

02:03:15 --> 02:03:19
really depends what do you want to put

02:03:17 --> 02:03:20
on a flash card depends on what

02:03:18 --> 02:03:22
information do you want uploaded to your

02:03:19 --> 02:03:23
brain a few tips on this if you're still

02:03:21 --> 02:03:25
struggling first ly the most important

02:03:22 --> 02:03:27
hing to do is to scope the subject

02:03:24 --> 02:03:29
alked about this extensively in the

02:03:26 --> 02:03:30
previous skillshare class about how to

02:03:28 --> 02:03:32
study for exams you should check that

02:03:29 --> 02:03:35
out scope the subject first figure out

02:03:31 --> 02:03:37
kind of the general tree of your subject

02:03:34 --> 02:03:39
and the general branches before worrying

02:03:36 --> 02:03:41
about the kind of the details on the

02:03:38 --> 02:03:42
leaves because an is very good at the

02:03:40 --> 02:03:43
details on the leaves and the leaves

02:03:41 --> 02:03:45
it's not very good at the branches and

02:03:42 --> 02:03:47
the big tree so scope the subject

02:03:44 --> 02:03:48
understand the big tree understand where

02:03:46 --> 02:03:50
stuff fits in and then once you've done

02:03:47 --> 02:03:53
that you can kind of break your subject

02:03:49 --> 02:03:54
down into the relevant bits and while

02:03:52 --> 02:03:56
you're doing that you're looking through

02:03:53 --> 02:03:58
past exam papers if you don't have past

02:03:55 --> 02:04:00
exam papers ask someone a year older

02:03:57 --> 02:04:02
than you what what they had in their

02:03:59 --> 02:04:04
exam you will get some kind of idea

02:04:01 --> 02:04:05
what's in the exam there is literally

02:04:03 --> 02:04:07
zero excuse if you're sitting there

02:04:04 --> 02:04:09
thinking oh but my school don't give out

02:04:06 --> 02:04:10
exam papers like there are so many

02:04:08 --> 02:04:12
sources of information out there there's

02:04:10 --> 02:04:14
the internet there's your professors

02:04:11 --> 02:04:15
your lecturers your teachers students in

02:04:13 --> 02:04:16
your year students in the year above

02:04:14 --> 02:04:17
students two years above there are

02:04:15 --> 02:04:18
thousands of sources of information

02:04:16 --> 02:04:20
where you can find out what's going to

02:04:18 --> 02:04:22
be on the exam basically and therefore

02:04:19 --> 02:04:24
you can kind of build your flash card

02:04:21 --> 02:04:26
strategy around what you know is going

02:04:23 --> 02:04:27
to sort of come up on the exam if you're

02:04:25 --> 02:04:29
a medical student lots of medical

02:04:26 --> 02:04:30
students use anky I mean the way that I

02:04:28 --> 02:04:32
would do it for clinical school is that

02:04:29 --> 02:04:33
I would be doing question Banks online

02:04:31 --> 02:04:35
there's loads of question Banks

02:04:32 --> 02:04:36
available I use past medicine and past

02:04:34 --> 02:04:38
est personally but there's loads of

02:04:35 --> 02:04:40
ones for the usml and if I got stuff

02:04:37 --> 02:04:42
wrong I'd be looking it up in for

02:04:39 --> 02:04:43
example a textbook or Wikipedia and I'd

02:04:41 --> 02:04:45
be turning that into a flash card

02:04:43 --> 02:04:47
because I know it's something that I got

02:04:44 --> 02:04:48
wrong and we learn best when we are

02:04:46 --> 02:04:50
learning based on the things we got

02:04:47 --> 02:04:52
wrong there's no real Point making flash

02:04:49 --> 02:04:53
cards on the things that we get right

02:04:51 --> 02:04:55
because then we just end up with way too

02:04:52 --> 02:04:57
many flash cards and it kind of becomes

02:04:54 --> 02:04:58
an exercise in futility so yeah I know

02:04:56 --> 02:04:59
that doesn't really answer the question

02:04:57 --> 02:05:01
but so many people asked what should I

02:04:58 --> 02:05:03
put on my flash cards and the answer is

02:05:00 --> 02:05:04
dude I'm so sorry but it just massively

02:05:02 --> 02:05:05
depends on absolutely everything most

02:05:03 --> 02:05:07
importantly what are you going to be

02:05:04 --> 02:05:08
xamined on what are you trying to

02:05:06 --> 02:05:10
upload to your memory that's kind of the

02:05:07 --> 02:05:11
main point so thank you for watching and

02:05:09 --> 02:05:13
I'll see you in the next video all right

02:05:10 --> 02:05:14
so one of the most common questions that

02:05:12 --> 02:05:16
we get is how long should my flash card

02:05:13 --> 02:05:18
be and this is controversial right it's

02:05:15 --> 02:05:20
controversial because there is a

02:05:17 --> 02:05:22
spectrum there are some people who are

02:05:19 --> 02:05:25
on the sort of minimum information

02:05:22 --> 02:05:26
principle Spectrum end of the spectrum

02:05:24 --> 02:05:28
where they would be very staunch

02:05:25 --> 02:05:31
Believers in that there should only be

02:05:27 --> 02:05:32
one single fact per flash card like each

02:05:30 --> 02:05:34
flash card should only have one

02:05:31 --> 02:05:35
objective and that should be to only put

02:05:33 --> 02:05:37
one fact into your brain and if you have

02:05:34 --> 02:05:38
to learn 10 facts you create 10 flash

02:05:36 --> 02:05:40
cards because that's how it works then

02:05:37 --> 02:05:43
on the other end of the spectrum you've

02:05:39 --> 02:05:46
got people like me for certain exams who

02:05:42 --> 02:05:48
would put entire paragraphs or for examp

02:05:45 --> 02:05:49
like even I think some of my flash cards

02:05:47 --> 02:05:50
had like four paragraphs in them where I

02:05:48 --> 02:05:53
was just kind of committing those four

02:05:49 --> 02:05:54
paragraphs to memory but in that context

02:05:52 --> 02:05:56
I Was preparing for an essay exam where

02:05:53 --> 02:05:57
I wanted all those flash cards to be

02:05:55 --> 02:05:58
uploaded to my brain and the reason I

02:05:56 --> 02:06:00
had them as paragraphs rather than just

02:05:57 --> 02:06:02
as bullet points is because I wanted the

02:05:59 --> 02:06:05
writing style of those paragraphs to

02:06:01 --> 02:06:07
also be uploaded to my brain so it kind

02:06:04 --> 02:06:09
of depends on what what you're aiming

02:06:06 --> 02:06:10
for I would say in general if we're

02:06:08 --> 02:06:12
asking the question how long should a

02:06:09 --> 02:06:14
flash card be the answer would be the

02:06:11 --> 02:06:15
shorter the better in general especially

02:06:13 --> 02:06:17
if you're a beginner at using Anki I

02:06:14 --> 02:06:19
would stick to the minimum information

02:06:16 --> 02:06:21
principle which is one fact per flash

02:06:18 --> 02:06:23
card because you can't really go wrong

02:06:20 --> 02:06:24
with that it's when you start adding way

02:06:22 --> 02:06:27
too many flags to your facts to your

02:06:23 --> 02:06:29
flash cards that it becomes such a

02:06:26 --> 02:06:31
ballach to get through that you will

02:06:28 --> 02:06:33
just stop doing it and like they say in

02:06:30 --> 02:06:34
like the Fitness World you know the best

02:06:32 --> 02:06:36
workout routine is the one that you

02:06:33 --> 02:06:37
follow like there's no point trying to

02:06:35 --> 02:06:39
find the perfect workout routine because

02:06:36 --> 02:06:40
chances are you won't follow it the best

02:06:38 --> 02:06:43
one is the one that you'll follow if you

02:06:39 --> 02:06:45
start trying to sort of extend anky too

02:06:42 --> 02:06:46
much especially as a beginner in it you

02:06:44 --> 02:06:48
will end up just giving up and not using

02:06:45 --> 02:06:50
it it's like you know going to the gym

02:06:47 --> 02:06:51
and trying to bench 100 kg on your very

02:06:49 --> 02:06:52
first attempt like it's it's just not

02:06:50 --> 02:06:53
going to happen you're going to become

02:06:51 --> 02:06:55
demoralized and you won't be able to do

02:06:52 --> 02:06:57
it consistently whereas if you stick to

02:06:54 --> 02:06:59
the minimum information principle of one

02:06:56 --> 02:07:00
factoid per flash card you can't really

02:06:58 --> 02:07:02
go wrong with that that's what I would

02:06:59 --> 02:07:04
recommend that's a general advice and

02:07:01 --> 02:07:05
over time as you get better at using

02:07:03 --> 02:07:07
ankey and more experienced you can then

02:07:04 --> 02:07:09
start modifying your flash cards to add

02:07:06 --> 02:07:10
a little bit more information to them if

02:07:08 --> 02:07:12
you have to because remember as we've

02:07:09 --> 02:07:13
discussed in the previous sections you

02:07:11 --> 02:07:15
can always modify your flash cards and

02:07:12 --> 02:07:16
it's very easy to do and it's something

02:07:14 --> 02:07:18
that you absolutely should be doing so

02:07:15 --> 02:07:20
that's my take on the topic but we're

now going to include some interview

02:07:19 --> 02:07:23
footage of a discussion that I had with

02:07:20 --> 02:07:25
prer and Carter both of them use Anki

02:07:22 --> 02:07:27
very extensively to absolutely smash

02:07:24 --> 02:07:29
their exams Carter came like 99.99

02:07:26 --> 02:07:31
percentile in the MCAT from medical

02:07:28 --> 02:07:33
school prag like really really well in

ou're a medical student I don't think

02:22:59 --> 02:23:03
in this video we are tackling the

02:23:00 --> 02:23:05
question of how do you avoid flash card

02:23:02 --> 02:23:07
overload and this is something that we

02:23:04 --> 02:23:09
are all going to uh experience when we

02:23:06 --> 02:23:11
start using ankey sometimes you know

02:23:08 --> 02:23:12
even with the best of intentions you

02:23:10 --> 02:23:14
just find that you've made a lot of

02:23:11 --> 02:23:16
cards and the cards just start to pile

02:23:13 --> 02:23:17
up like maybe there's one day where you

02:23:15 --> 02:23:19
miss your reviews and suddenly you've

02:23:16 --> 02:23:20
got 400 cards to do the next day and

02:23:18 --> 02:23:22
then you think oh 400 cards I can't

02:23:19 --> 02:23:23
be honest with this and then you skip

02:23:21 --> 02:23:26
your review and then you got 600 and now

02:23:22 --> 02:23:27
you're being overloaded by flash cards

02:23:25 --> 02:23:29
there's a few kind of tips that we can

02:23:26 --> 02:23:31
use to get around this firstly and most

02:23:28 --> 02:23:34
importantly if you can consistently do

02:23:30 --> 02:23:36
your flash cards every single day then

02:23:33 --> 02:23:37
you will not be overloaded by flash card

02:23:35 --> 02:23:40
that's just the first thing to say

02:23:37 --> 02:23:41
secondly you want to avoid making flash

02:23:39 --> 02:23:43
cards on the stuff that you know you

02:23:40 --> 02:23:44
already know especially at the start

02:23:42 --> 02:23:46
especially if you're not following my

02:23:43 --> 02:23:48
advice and kind of making flash cards

02:23:45 --> 02:23:49
from day one of med school you're going

02:23:47 --> 02:23:51
to make loads of flash cards on things

02:23:48 --> 02:23:53
just because you like the idea of making

02:23:50 --> 02:23:54
a flash card and it feels like a safety

02:23:52 --> 02:23:56
blanket and you think oh I couldn't

02:23:53 --> 02:23:58
possibly know this without a flash card

02:23:55 --> 02:23:59
but when it comes to flash cards we

02:23:57 --> 02:24:01
don't want to be creating that many

02:23:58 --> 02:24:02
because the more flash cards we create

02:24:00 --> 02:24:05
Let's see we create 100 flash cards

02:24:01 --> 02:24:06
every lecture and we've got 100 lectures

02:24:04 --> 02:24:08
that is way too many flash cards to

02:24:05 --> 02:24:10
handle instead we should probably create

02:24:07 --> 02:24:11
maybe 10 to 15 flash cards per lecture

02:24:09 --> 02:24:13
ven if it's a lecture that's 1 hour

02:24:10 --> 02:24:15
long because the rest of the information

02:24:12 --> 02:24:16
probably doesn't need a flash card it

02:24:14 --> 02:24:18
doesn't require us to memorize it it

02:24:15 --> 02:24:20
probably requires us to understand it

02:24:17 --> 02:24:21
and as we've talked about Anki is not

02:24:19 --> 02:24:24
great for understanding anky is

02:24:21 --> 02:24:26
wonderful for memorization so yeah

02:24:23 --> 02:24:28
number one do it consistently number two

02:24:25 --> 02:24:30
avoid making too many flash cards

02:24:27 --> 02:24:31
thirdly even with the best of intentions

02:24:29 --> 02:24:33
and the best strategy the flash cards

02:24:30 --> 02:24:34
can pile up especially towards the exams

02:24:32 --> 02:24:35
when you've gotten through all your

02:24:33 --> 02:24:37
syllabus and you've got a few hundred

02:24:34 --> 02:24:39
flash cards to review each day one way

02:24:36 --> 02:24:41
to avoid overload in that front is just

02:24:38 --> 02:24:42
kind of split up the studying session so

02:24:40 --> 02:24:43
what I would do is I would do a little

02:24:41 --> 02:24:44
bit in the morning then a bit in the

02:24:42 --> 02:24:46
afternoon then a bit in the evening then

02:24:44 --> 02:24:48
a bit before bed while I'm lying in bed

02:24:45 --> 02:24:49
with a dark mode on on ankey using an

02:24:47 --> 02:24:51
add-on that kind of makes me swipe

02:24:48 --> 02:24:52
through flash cards and I just make sure

02:24:50 --> 02:24:54
I get all of them done by staggering

02:24:51 --> 02:24:55
them throughout the day so that would be

02:24:53 --> 02:24:57
one other way of doing it and now we've

so it's very it's very useful but it's

called a load balancer

02:27:27 --> 02:27:33

02:27:30 --> 02:27:35
basically will look at how many reviews

02:27:34 --> 02:27:40

02:27:50 --> 02:27:55

's a caveat here I think pre-made

ally bad if you're the type of person

rself have already gone through that

02:57:22 --> 02:57:27
verall let's talk about pros and cons

02:57:24 --> 02:57:29
o as PR said the benefit of using

02:57:26 --> 02:57:31
pre-made decks is clearly that you then

02:57:28 --> 02:57:32
don't have to make your own flash cards

02:57:30 --> 02:57:34
but there are a few huge drawbacks

02:57:31 --> 02:57:36
firstly there is the massive risk of

02:57:33 --> 02:57:38
lash card overload if you make the

02:57:35 --> 02:57:39
mistake of not suspending basically all

02:57:37 --> 02:57:41
of the cards in a pre-made deck and then

02:57:38 --> 02:57:43
unsuspending the ones as you study as

02:57:40 --> 02:57:45
you study the topic you can see that

02:57:42 --> 02:57:46
list of 20,000 cards and think I'm never

02:57:44 --> 02:57:47
going to do this and then you just throw

02:57:45 --> 02:57:49
your laptop in the bin because you've

02:57:46 --> 02:57:51
given up on anky that's what we want to

02:57:48 --> 02:57:52
do if we're using pre-made decks we want

02:57:50 --> 02:57:54
o be using them effectively and

02:57:51 --> 02:57:56
appropriately Point number two is that

02:57:53 --> 02:57:58
yes it does save you time using a

02:57:55 --> 02:58:00
pre-made deck but the research shows

02:57:57 --> 02:58:03
that it is probably a bit more effective

02:57:59 --> 02:58:04
to create your own cards overall in my

02:58:02 --> 02:58:08
opinion and in most people's opinion the

02:58:04 --> 02:58:09
time you save from using a pre-made deck

02:58:07 --> 02:58:11
is more than made up for the fact that

02:58:08 --> 02:58:13
using a pre-made deck is slightly less

02:58:10 --> 02:58:14
efficient is or rather is is is slightly

02:58:12 --> 02:58:17
less effective than creating your own

02:58:13 --> 02:58:21
cards I would much rather have 80%

02:58:16 --> 02:58:23
Effectiveness and Save myself time that

02:58:20 --> 02:58:25
I don't have to make these 10,000 cards

02:58:22 --> 02:58:26
then have 100% Effectiveness but have to

02:58:24 --> 02:58:28

02:58:25 --> 02:58:30
creating these flash cards so overall I

02:58:27 --> 02:58:32
think the the benefit of pre-made decks

02:58:29 --> 02:58:34
vastly outweighs the negative side of

02:58:31 --> 02:58:35
them but it is important to understand

02:58:33 --> 02:58:37
the negative side and third it's really

02:58:34 --> 02:58:39
important to remember that if you are

02:58:36 --> 02:58:40

02:58:38 --> 02:58:42
